 If you prefer to create the files directly, use `~/.copilot/hooks/`, or `$COPILOT_HOME/hooks/` when `COPILOT_HOME` is set, for the configuration and script.
+
+## Control which hooks run
+
+In Copilot sessions, repository hooks require a [trusted working folder](/docs/editing/workspaces/workspace-trust.md). Hooks declared by custom agents run only when every folder in the session is trusted. The **Hooks** page shows hooks that cannot run with a **Won't run** badge and an explanation.
+
+To turn off repository and custom-agent hooks, set ``chat.useHooks`` to `false` in **User** settings. Copilot uses the User setting or an enforced `ChatHooks` policy, not the workspace setting. An open chat applies the change from its next message, after any running background subagents finish.
+
+> [!IMPORTANT]
+> This setting is not a global hook switch. User hooks, policy hooks, and hooks that the runtime loads from installed plugin folders still run. Administrators can use [managed hook-source controls](/docs/enterprise/manage-ai-settings.md#restrict-hook-sources) to restrict which sources are eligible.

-* `chat.useHooks` controls Local execution. These settings do not configure the shared Copilot runtime.
+* ``chat.useHooks`` controls Local execution and also [controls some Copilot hook sources](#control-which-hooks-run). `chat.hookFilesLocations` and `chat.useClaudeHooks` do not configure the shared Copilot runtime.
 * The Local parser accepts PascalCase and Copilot event aliases, command aliases, and nested Claude command groups. It ignores matcher values and still sends Local payloads.

 ## Troubleshoot hooks
 
 1. Confirm the **Session Target**, installed runtime version, and hook source.
+1. Open **Hooks** in the Agent Customizations editor. Review any **Won't run** explanation and notices about hook files that could not load. For a deprecated Local-format hook, follow [Migrate with Agent](/docs/agent-customization/migrate-customizations.md#migrate-local-format-hooks-with-the-agent).
 1. Check whether another user, repository, settings, agent, or plugin source contributes the same command.
 1. Run the script directly with a non-sensitive event fixture. Check its exit status and exact JSON output, including malformed-input behavior.
 1. Start a disposable session and confirm the hook fires for the expected event and tool. A file shown in the editor is not proof that its script executed.
 1. Check dependencies, path quoting, `cwd`, and permissions in the execution environment. Test timeouts separately from command errors.
 
-Use the [agent debug logs](/docs/agents/agent-troubleshooting/chat-debug-view.md#agent-debug-logs-panel) and the provider's diagnostics to investigate. Avoid logging complete prompts, arguments, results, or environment variables that might contain sensitive data.
+In a live Copilot chat, a blocked prompt shows the hook's reason instead of a model reply. Tool calls denied by a hook show the reason and are identified as blocked by hooks rather than ordinary failures. When the runtime identifies a failed hook's source, the diagnostic also names its configuration file. A warning about unusable output means that the runtime did not use the reported output, not that it disabled the hook.
+
+Use ``chat.hooks.showWarnings`` to show hook execution errors and warnings. It is on by default in Insiders. **Disable Hook Warnings** hides these diagnostics without disabling hooks. Blocked-prompt errors remain visible even with warnings off.
+
+Some hook notices and blocked-tool details are not retained when you reopen a chat. Use the [agent debug logs](/docs/agents/agent-troubleshooting/chat-debug-view.md#agent-debug-logs-panel) and the provider's diagnostics to investigate. Avoid logging complete prompts, arguments, results, or environment variables that might contain sensitive data.
 
 For an old Local setup, **Chat: Configure Hooks** lists its files, and the **GitHub Copilot Chat Hooks** output channel shows its command errors.
 
```

### [MODIFICADO] `microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/language-models.md`
- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agent-customization/language-models
- **Hash Anterior**: `1c1c910a42fed0de6fd0b5c64f369ebcc4be83c5d1a6a2ec85874229deef9d12`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `b9fdcd064f927570d79f7594f5a122fa627ea90a1f13913ed88211b5ebe288c3`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/language-models.md (prev: 1c1c910a)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agent-customization/language-models.md (new: b9fdcd06)
@@ -106,6 +106,95 @@
 1. In the model picker, select **Copilot Fusion**, then choose an available tier: **Standard**, **High**, or **Max**.
 
 Enabling the setting doesn't grant access to Fusion tiers. If the service doesn't offer a Fusion tier that your account can run, the picker continues to show **Auto** instead.
+
+## Use model routers
+
+`feature(model-routers)`
+
+Unlike Auto or Fusion, which choose models for you, a model router uses your own model choices and routing criteria. Define routes for recurring work, such as a faster model for small edits and a reasoning model for multi-file changes.
+
+> [!NOTE]
+> Model routers are available in VS Code Insiders for [Copilot harness sessions](/docs/agents/run/agent-harnesses.md#copilot) and are off by default.
+
+### Create and select a router
+
+1. Enable ``chat.copilot.modelRouter.enabled`` in the Settings editor.
+
+1. Open a local folder in the [Agents window](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md) and start a **Copilot** chat.
+
+1. In the model picker, select **Routers** > **Create a Router**, or **New Router** if you already have one.
+
+1. The action adds `/model-router` to your draft. Describe your routing preferences and send the request:
+
+    ```prompt
+    /model-router Use a reasoning model for debugging and multi-file changes, and a faster model for small edits. Choose models available to my account.
+    ```
+
+1. Review the router and any file or tool approvals. The skill validates it with `check_model_router`. Reopen **Routers** and select your router.
+
+Send a task. The routing line identifies the route, model, and effort. The response footer identifies the answering model.
+
+Open a router's **Details** to inspect its routes, model configurations, and switching mode. Select its file name to edit it. New and changed files are detected without reloading.
+
+### Write a router file
+
+Alternatively, create `<name>.model-router.yaml` in `~/.agents/model-routers/` on macOS or Linux, or `%USERPROFILE%\.agents\model-routers\` on Windows. Use lowercase letters, digits, and single hyphens for `<name>`, such as `daily-driver`.
+
+Routers in `~/.copilot/model-routers/` are also supported. For duplicate names, `~/.agents/` takes precedence.
+
+Adapt this example's models to your account, manually or with `/model-router`:
+
+```yaml
+version: 1
+display-name: Daily Driver
+description: Reasoning for multi-file work, faster responses for small edits.
+routes:
+  - name: build
+    model: opus
+    effort: high
+    when: Debugging, implementing features, or changing behavior across files.
+    guidance: Inspect the relevant code before editing. Run the relevant tests after changes.
+  - name: quick
+    model: haiku
+    effort: low
+    when: Small, well-defined edits, such as renaming a local variable or updating a comment.
+```
+
+* **Models:** Use a family for its newest available released model, an exact ID for a particular version, or a preference list such as `[opus, haiku]`.
+* **Criteria:** Describe the work in `when`, with optional `examples` to distinguish requests. Put your preferred fallback first: an unclear first request uses the first available route.
+* **Effort:** Set a supported `effort` level, or let the router choose when routing a request. Top-level `effort: off` keeps model defaults and ignores route effort.
+* **Context:** Set `context-size: long` for a larger supported window. Once a chat uses long context, subsequent models retain it where supported.
+
+After manual edits, ask `/model-router` to check for unavailable models, unsupported effort levels, and invalid YAML.
+
+### Control switching and route guidance
+
+By default, the first message after you select a router determines its route for the rest of the chat. To switch routes as the work changes, add at the top level:
+
+```yaml
+switching: auto
+```
+
+Short continuation replies generally keep the current route. Evaluate edited routing criteria in a new chat.
+
+A route's `guidance` instructs the main agent, including steps, [skills](/docs/agent-customization/agent-skills.md), or [custom agents](/docs/agent-customization/custom-agents.md) to use. Switching routes replaces that guidance. Running background subagents delay guidance changes until a routed turn after they finish.
+
+Top-level `guidance` influences routing decisions, not the agent's work. Put instructions such as running tests in route guidance. Guidance doesn't enforce workflows or permissions.
+
+Switching models resets the prompt cache and can increase [AI credit usage](/docs/agents/concepts/language-models.md#ai-credits-and-model-costs). Guidance changes, higher effort, and larger context windows can also add cost.
+
+### Check unexpected routing
+
+For unexpected choices, ask `/model-router` to review overlapping criteria and add representative examples. Models must be available to your account and permitted by organization policies.
+
+| Condition | Behavior |
+|---|---|
+| All models in a route are unavailable | The route is skipped. |
+| Router file is invalid, missing, or has no available models | The chat keeps its current model and shows a notice. |
+| Routing is unavailable for the first request | The first available route is used, with a notice. |
+| Routing fails for a later request | The current route is kept. |
+
+A fallback isn't necessarily the best match for your task.
 
 ## Use on-device models
 
```

 * **Custom agents with Local harness-specific behavior**: use **Migrate with Agent** when an agent depends on Local harness `feature(local-harness)` tool names, tool sets, hooks, or other behavior that needs semantic changes.
 * **Tool sets**: deprecated and not supported in the Copilot harness. [Replace tool-set references](#replace-tool-sets) with tools configured for the workflow.
 * **Chat participants**: deprecated and not supported in the Copilot harness. [Replace participant capabilities](#replace-chat-participants) with plugins that combine skills, MCP tools, and, when needed, MCP Apps.
-* **Hooks**: there is no automatic hook migration. Follow [Migrate hooks](#migrate-hooks) to review discovery, command configuration, script inputs, and output decisions.
+* **Hooks**: there is no automatic hook conversion. Use [Migrate with Agent](#migrate-local-format-hooks-with-the-agent) for detected Local-format files. For other sources or manual migration, follow [Migrate hooks](#migrate-hooks) to review discovery, command configuration, script inputs, and output decisions.
 * **Plugins and marketplaces**: manage these separately from the migration list. See [Discover and install plugins](/docs/agent-customization/agent-plugins.md#discover-and-install-plugins).
 * **Extension-provided customizations**: extensions can provide tools, MCP servers, custom agents, skills, and instructions. Keep the contributing extension installed. Extension tools are available only in editor chat while the extension runs. Learn how to [manage extension tools for the Copilot harness](/docs/agents/run/tools.md#add-extension-tools-for-the-copilot-harness).
 
@@ -285,12 +285,26 @@
 
 The [Copilot hooks reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/hooks-reference) documents the shared configuration and command-hook payloads. The [Local hooks reference](/docs/agents/reference/hooks-reference.md) describes the source format for an existing Local hook.
 
+### Migrate Local-format hooks with the agent
+
+The Agent Customizations editor marks detected Local-format hooks as **Needs attention**. This warning identifies a compatibility risk, not proof that the hook is broken.
+
+1. Complete [Prepare hooks for migration](#prepare-hooks-for-migration) before starting.
+1. In **Hooks**, open the affected hook and select **Review Migrations**. Alternatively, [open the migration list](#open-the-migration-list) directly.
+1. Review the **Local-Format Hooks** group, which lists your detected workspace and user hook files.
+1. Select **Migrate with Agent** in a local **Copilot** session. The agent reviews the hook file and its scripts together and asks before changing the intended behavior.
+1. Review the resulting changes and [verify the migration](#verify-the-migration) before relying on the hook.
+
+Unlike a file move, hook migration can update the configuration and scripts in place. The recovery bundle includes the hook file and directly referenced scripts within the workspace or hook folder. Keep your own backup of other dependencies. For plugin or administrator-managed hooks, work with the publisher or administrator instead of changing an installed copy.
+
+To migrate manually, use the preparation, source inventory, and conversion guidance below.
+
 ### Prepare hooks for migration
 
 Before editing a configuration or starting a Copilot session:
 
 1. Back up the original configuration **and** its scripts, including user settings that are not in source control.
-1. Record which hooks are enabled, disabled, or excluded by Local discovery settings. `chat.useHooks`, `chat.useClaudeHooks`, and `chat.hookFilesLocations` do not preserve those choices in the Copilot harness. A previously ignored file can become active without being moved.
+1. Record which hooks are enabled, disabled, or excluded by Local discovery settings. In Copilot sessions, ``chat.useHooks`` uses User settings or policy to control repository and custom-agent hooks, but does not stop user hooks, policy hooks, or hooks loaded directly from plugin folders. `chat.useClaudeHooks` and `chat.hookFilesLocations` do not configure Copilot discovery. A previously ignored file can become active without being moved. Review [hook execution controls](/docs/agent-customization/hooks.md#control-which-hooks-run).
 1. Keep unvalidated configurations outside discovered sources. For example, stage a standalone file with a `.json.disabled` suffix, and use a new script filename rather than overwriting the original. For inline settings, prepare the new `hooks` entries separately before merging them.
 1. Preserve intentionally disabled hooks using the destination's controls. Do not activate the original and converted configurations together.
 
@@ -343,7 +357,7 @@
 | Local event | Copilot event | Behavior to verify |
 |-------------|---------------|--------------------|
 | `SessionStart` | `sessionStart` | Copilot includes new and resumed sessions. Do not assume `source` is always `new`. |
-| `UserPromptSubmit` | `userPromptSubmitted` | Runs for a submitted prompt. Command-hook output is ignored for this event; programmatic SDK prompt rewriting is not a shell-hook feature. |
+| `UserPromptSubmit` | `userPromptSubmitted` | Runs before the submitted prompt reaches the model. Verify any prompt-blocking behavior and the reason shown in chat. |
 | `PreToolUse` | `preToolUse` | Runs before a tool executes. Verify the tool name, argument schema, and permission result. |
 | `PostToolUse` | `postToolUse` | Runs after a successful tool. Copilot has a separate `postToolUseFailure` event. |
 | `PreCompact` | `preCompact` | Copilot documents manual and automatic triggers. It is a notification, not a way to prevent compaction. |

 Selecting a Dev Container changes the execution environment, not the harness. For supported hosts, interfaces, and setup, see [run a session in a Dev Container](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md#run-a-session-in-a-dev-container).
 
-For sessions that offer folder and worktree options, code isolation controls which working directory receives changes. Folder isolation applies edits directly to your current workspace, including its uncommitted changes. Worktree isolation gives the session a separate [Git worktree](/docs/sourcecontrol/branches-worktrees.md#understanding-worktrees) based on committed Git state. Dev Container sessions work directly in the container workspace and don't support **New Worktree**.
+For sessions that offer folder and worktree options, code isolation controls which working directory receives changes. Folder isolation applies edits directly to your current workspace. Worktree isolation gives the session a separate working directory based on committed Git state. For the comparison, requirements, and setup in either interface, see [Work with agents in worktrees](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md).
 
 A worktree is a Git code-isolation boundary, not a security boundary. It does not restrict commands, network access, or access to files outside the worktree. Use [agent sandboxing](/docs/agents/run/agent-sandboxing.md) for operating system-level file system and network restrictions.
 
```

--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/language-models.md (prev: f066c53d)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/concepts/language-models.md (new: 4d13b73d)
@@ -93,6 +93,16 @@
 
 Learn how to [use Copilot Fusion](/docs/agent-customization/language-models.md#use-copilot-fusion). For details about orchestration, see [GitHub Copilot Fusion](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/models/copilot-fusion) in the GitHub documentation.
 
+### Model routers
+
+`feature(model-routers)`
+
+A model router selects from routes you define for recurring work. Each route describes the requests it should handle and the model to use, with optional thinking effort, context size, and guidance for the agent. Unlike Auto or Fusion, which choose models for you, a router uses your own model choices and routing criteria.
+
+Use a router when you already have model preferences for different tasks and want to reuse them. For example, choose a reasoning model for multi-file changes and a faster model for small edits. Routing and guidance are model-driven, not guarantees about the result or its cost.
+
+Model routers are available in VS Code Insiders for Copilot harness sessions. Learn how to [create and use a model router](/docs/agent-customization/language-models.md#use-model-routers).
+
 ### AI credits and model costs
 
 GitHub Copilot plans use [AI credits](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/billing/usage-based-billing-for-individuals) to measure usage. Consumption depends on the model's rates and the tokens processed, not only the number of messages you send. Check your plan for its allowance and usage limits.
```

 A fork can open as another chat in the same session or as a new session, depending on the harness. Forking a conversation isn't the same as creating a Git branch or a separate worktree. For example, a fork of a Copilot worktree session continues to use the original worktree.
 
 > [!IMPORTANT]
-> Separate conversations don't guarantee separate files. If two chats or sessions use the same folder or worktree, their edits affect the same files. Choose separate worktrees for nested or top-level sessions, where supported, when changes must stay separate. Learn how to [choose where an agent makes changes](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md#choose-where-an-agent-makes-changes).
+> Separate conversations don't guarantee separate files. If two chats or sessions use the same folder or worktree, their edits affect the same files. Choose separate worktrees for nested or top-level sessions, where supported, when changes must stay separate. Learn how to [choose where an agent makes changes](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#choose-folder-or-worktree-isolation).
 
 Sessions can run in parallel and keep running when you switch between them. To return an existing conversation and affected workspace files to an earlier point instead of branching the conversation, see [Restore a checkpoint](/docs/agents/run/review-code-edits.md#restore-a-checkpoint).
 
```

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/run/agents-window.md (prev: 1b08904e)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/run/agents-window.md (new: 88727473)
@@ -68,27 +68,19 @@
 
 ### Choose where an agent makes changes
 
-For a Git workspace you can choose between applying changes in the current branch or a new worktree. Use the isolation dropdown above the input to choose where the agent edits files.
+For a Git workspace, use the isolation dropdown above the input to choose where the agent edits files:
+
+* **Branch**: Work directly in the selected folder. If you choose another local branch, switching branches requires a clean working tree.
+* **New Worktree**: Work in a separate directory and branch. Use the branch picker to choose the base branch before sending the prompt. Choosing a base branch doesn't switch the branch in your active workspace.
 
 ![Screenshot showing the isolation dropdown with Branch and New Worktree options above the input.](../images/agent-harnesses/agents-window-new-worktree-2.png)
 
-| Aspect | **Branch** | **New Worktree** |
-|--------|------------|------------------|
-| Best for | Working with your current files and uncommitted changes | Independent tasks running in parallel |
-| Where edits go | Your current folder | A separate [Git worktree](/docs/sourcecontrol/branches-worktrees.md#understanding-worktrees) |
-| Starting point | Your current files, or another existing local branch | Committed files from the selected base branch |
-| Requirements | A clean working tree **when switching branches** | A Git repository with at least one commit |
-| Uncommitted and untracked files | Available in the current folder | Not copied from the current folder |
-| Integration | Edits are already in your selected folder | Integrate the reviewed worktree branch afterward |
-
-Choosing a base branch for **New Worktree** doesn't switch the branch in your active workspace.
-
-Git-ignored files, including dependencies and local configuration, are also absent from new worktrees by default. Run your project's setup or [configure which ignored files to include](#configure-isolation-defaults).
+For the folder-versus-worktree comparison, Git requirements, and dependency setup, see [Work with agents in worktrees](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md). To change the initial choice, [configure isolation defaults](#configure-isolation-defaults).
 
 Dev Container sessions work directly in the container workspace and don't support **New Worktree**. See [Run a session in a Dev Container](#run-a-session-in-a-dev-container).
 
 > [!IMPORTANT]
-> Worktrees isolate working files, not the agent's access to your machine or external services. Either isolation mode supports the permission levels offered by your harness. For operating system-level restrictions, configure [agent sandboxing](/docs/agents/run/agent-sandboxing.md) or use [cloud sessions](/docs/agents/run/cloud-sandbox-sessions.md).
+> Worktrees isolate working files, not the agent's access to your machine or external services. Review the [permission and sandboxing considerations](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#choose-folder-or-worktree-isolation) before starting a task.
 
 ### Save and resume drafts
 
@@ -256,6 +248,8 @@
 
 After combining changes from multiple sessions, rerun the relevant checks on the combined result. Keep the sessions available until that validation is complete, then [mark finished sessions as done](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md#finish-and-restore-sessions).

 ### Create a pull request
 
 If the session doesn't already have a pull request:
@@ -330,7 +324,15 @@
 
 ### Run a session in a Dev Container
 
-Use a Dev Container to run the agent with your project's tools and dependencies. This is supported in the desktop app for local folders and folders on configured SSH, Tunnel, or WSL hosts, but not in the browser-based window.
+Use a Dev Container to run the agent with tools and dependencies inside a container rather than directly on its host. Containers can run on this computer or on a configured SSH, Tunnel, or WSL host. These workflows aren't supported in the VS Code for the Web.
+
+Choose the starting point that fits your task:
+
+* [Use your project's Dev Container](#use-your-projects-dev-container) to work with its existing files and configuration.
+* [Create a container without a project](#create-a-container-without-a-project-experimental) for a fresh Ubuntu workspace.
+* [Ask the agent to continue in a new container](#ask-the-agent-to-continue-in-a-new-container-experimental) to hand off a task to a new session.
+
+#### Use your project's Dev Container
 
 Before starting:
 
@@ -350,6 +352,45 @@
 
 Restarting an existing container is different from rebuilding or replacing it. To keep container-stored chat sessions across replacements, enable ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.stateVolume.enabled`` before creating the container. See [Preserve chat sessions across rebuilds](/docs/devcontainers/create-dev-container.md#preserve-chat-sessions-across-rebuilds) `feature(dev-container-state-volume)`, especially the warning about the first rebuild of an existing container.
 
+#### Create a container without a project (Experimental)
+
+Create a standalone Ubuntu container when you need a fresh workspace without an existing project folder or `devcontainer.json`. Its files stay in a Docker volume on the machine that runs the container.
+
+Before starting, [install and run Docker](/docs/devcontainers/containers.md#installation) on the intended host, with its CLI on `PATH`. For a remote host, [connect to it](/docs/agents/run/remote-agent-sessions.md) first. For WSL, Docker must be available inside the distribution.
+
+Enable ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.environments.enabled``, which is off by default. Keep ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.enabled`` and ``chat.remoteAgentHostsEnabled`` enabled.
+
+1. Run **Agents: Manage Remote Environments** from the Command Palette (``Shortcut: workbench.action.showCommands``).
+1. In the search box's **Filter** menu, choose **Group By > Type** to show the **Dev Containers** group.
+1. Select **Create Dev Container** on the **Dev Containers** group. If prompted, choose **This Computer** or a connected host.
+
+    To create the container on a specific connected host, you can instead use the create action on that host's row.
+
+1. After the container connects, select **New Session** and choose it in the **Environment** picker.
+1. Choose an available harness and submit your prompt.
+
+Standalone containers persist independently of their sessions. The idle timeout and session cleanup described for project-configured containers don't apply. Disconnecting or marking a session as done doesn't delete the container's files.
+
+> [!CAUTION]
+> To remove a standalone container, use **Delete** on its row in **Remote Environments**. This deletes the container and its Docker volumes, including workspace files. Save any work you need elsewhere first. Sessions that ran in the deleted container can no longer continue.
+
+To edit or debug the workspace, see [open a standalone container in the editor](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md#continue-between-the-chat-view-and-agents-window) for extension and WSL requirements.
+
+#### Ask the agent to continue in a new container (Experimental)
+
+To let an agent create a standalone container and continue work there, [enable standalone containers](#create-a-container-without-a-project-experimental) and turn on ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.handoff.enabled``, which is off by default. This also provides the remote session tools. You don't need to separately enable ``chat.remoteSessions.tools.enabled``.
+
+In an [Agent Host-backed session](/docs/agents/concepts/agent-host.md), describe the task and where the container should run. For example:
+
+```prompt
+Create a Dev Container on this computer for a Python command-line tool,
+then continue implementing the tool in a new session there.
+```
+
+Review the container host and the new-session approval before proceeding. By default, the agent creates the container on the current session's host when it supports containers, otherwise on this computer. Name a connected host in your request when its location matters.
+
+The agent starts a new session in the container and links to it from the original conversation. It passes a task summary, not the full conversation history, and doesn't copy the original project's files into the new workspace. The container appears in **Remote Environments** and follows the same persistence and deletion rules as a manually created standalone container.
+
 ### Start a session from a pull request
 
 For a local [product.prodname_github]-backed workspace, start from an existing pull request to ask questions or continue its changes. The session includes the pull request details, changes, and comments, and uses an isolated worktree that tracks its branch.
@@ -427,7 +468,7 @@
 
 The ``sessions.useWorktree`` setting controls the initial isolation choice for a workspace that hasn't started a session before. After you start a session, VS Code remembers that workspace's isolation choice instead. The branch selection isn't remembered.
 
-Use ``git.worktreeIncludeFiles`` to [copy ignored files into new worktrees](/docs/sourcecontrol/branches-worktrees.md#include-files-when-creating-a-worktree). For large folders, ``git.worktreeSymlinkFolders`` (Experimental) can [symlink a folder instead](/docs/sourcecontrol/branches-worktrees.md#symlink-ignored-folders-when-creating-a-worktree). Changes through the symlink affect the original folder, so don't use it for files that must stay isolated.
+For dependencies and ignored files, see [Prepare a worktree](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#prepare-a-worktree).
 
 ## Limitations

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/run/chat-view.md (prev: 8f99e1db)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::docs/agents/run/chat-view.md (new: f614439b)
@@ -57,6 +57,34 @@
 
 If you already have a conversation running in the CLI, [continue it in the Chat view](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md#continue-a-cli-session-in-vs-code-experimental) with `/code` (Experimental).
 
+## Work in separate worktrees
+
+`feature(editor-worktrees)`
+
+Keep changes from parallel agent tasks separate without opening another editor window. Each chat can work in its own Git worktree, and the editor's folder location follows the chat you open. For isolation choices, starting code, and dependency setup, see [Work with agents in worktrees](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md).
+
+This workflow requires VS Code Insiders, a single local Git folder with at least one commit, and a harness that supports worktree isolation, such as Copilot.
+
+1. Enable ``chat.editorWindow.enableWorktree``, which is off by default.
+1. Start a new chat and choose your harness.
+1. Select **New Worktree** before sending the first prompt. If the chat input shows a folder picker, open it to select **New Worktree**.
+1. Describe the task and send your prompt. The chat works in a new worktree on its own branch, separate from your original folder.
+
+If the **Work in Parallel** callout appears, choose **Use New Worktree** to separate the task's changes, or **Continue in Folder** to share the folder. Later new chats use your answer as their default.
+
+### Switch between locations
+
+Open a chat from the sessions list to work in its location. Explorer and Source Control show that location's files, changes, and branch. To switch locations directly, select **Folder Location** in the Status Bar, then choose the original folder or an entry under **Worktrees**. Check the location and branch before editing or committing.
+
+![Screenshot showing the Folder Location menu with two worktrees and the current worktree identified in the menu and Status Bar.](../images/chat-view/editor-worktree-location-2026112301.png)
+
+> [!IMPORTANT]
+> Existing terminals keep their working directories when the editor changes location. Check a terminal's directory before running commands, or open a new terminal in the current location. A user-initiated location switch asks you to stop affected debug sessions and tasks with **Stop and Switch**. Automatic location changes can leave them running in the previous location.
+
+Switching locations doesn't integrate changes. When the task is ready, [review and integrate the worktree changes](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#review-and-integrate-changes).
+
+To reclaim disk space afterward, run **Chat: Open Worktree Cleanup** without leaving the editor window. Follow [Clean up session worktrees](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#clean-up-session-worktrees) to review what is preserved before removing worktrees.
+
 ## Work with the agent
 
 Keep the code and its checks beside the conversation as you work:
@@ -83,7 +111,7 @@
 
 ## Layout options
 
-The Chat view offers several layout options within the main VS Code window. Choose the layout that best fits your workflow:
+With the default chat layout, the Chat view offers several options within the main VS Code window. Choose the layout that best fits your workflow:
 
 * **Side bar** (default): select **New Chat (+)** > **New Chat**, or run the **Chat: New Chat** command. Best for keeping chat visible alongside your code.
 
@@ -91,9 +119,21 @@
 
 * **Separate window**: select **New Chat (+)** > **New Chat Window**, or run the **Chat: New Chat Window** command. Best for multi-monitor setups.
 
+### Open session chats in another area (Experimental)
+
+In VS Code Insiders, use ``chat.viewSessions.openChatIn`` to choose where conversations open when you select a session in a local editor window:
+
+* `chatPanel` (default): Open the conversation in the Chat view.
+* `chatArea`: Open the conversation in a dedicated chat area beside the Chat view, keeping it separate from your code editors.
+* `editorArea`: Open the conversation as an editor tab. Selecting an already-open session reveals its tab.
+
+Keep ``chat.viewSessions.orientation`` set to `sideBySide`. With either alternate layout, the Chat view shows the sessions list rather than the open conversation.
+
+Use `chatPanel` to return to the default layout or work with Local harness sessions.
+
 ## Interface overview
 
-The Chat view keeps the agent next to your code, so you can prompt, review, and edit in the same window. The Chat view has the following main areas:
+The Chat view keeps the agent next to your code, so you can prompt, review, and edit in the same window. With the default chat layout, it has the following main areas:
 
 1. **Sessions list**: at the top of the view, where you can view and manage your sessions for the current workspace. Learn more about the [sessions list](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md#find-and-resume-a-session).
 
@@ -108,7 +148,7 @@
 
 ### Sessions list layout
 
-Use the toggle control in the top-right corner of the Chat view to switch between two layouts:
+With the default chat layout, use the toggle control in the top-right corner of the Chat view to switch between two layouts:
 
 * **Compact**: The sessions list and conversation share the same panel. Select a session to open it, then use the back button to return to the list.
 * **Side-by-side**: The sessions list stays visible next to the conversation. Select a session to open its details. Use ``chat.viewSessions.orientation`` to configure the orientation.
@@ -117,7 +157,7 @@
 
 ### Switch chats within a session
 
-With a harness that supports multiple chats, a session can contain a main chat and nested sessions. In the **Sessions** view, expand the main chat row and select a nested session to open its conversation in the Chat view.
+With a harness that supports multiple chats, a session can contain a main chat and nested sessions. In the **Sessions** view, expand the main chat row and select a nested session to open its conversation.
 
 You can continue nested sessions created in the Agents window without starting a new conversation. For creation steps, navigation options, and interface-specific controls, see [Run multiple chats in a session](/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions.md#run-multiple-chats-in-a-session).
 
```

     ![Screenshot showing the Environment picker and its Manage entry.](../images/remote-agent-sessions/environment-picker-manage.png)
 
+#### Share this computer with the setup form (Experimental)
+
+To compare connection methods before starting sharing, enable ``sessions.chat.remoteEnvironments.sharingSetup``. This Experimental setting is off by default and requires the **This Computer** section to be enabled.
+
+On the host machine:
+
+1. [Open remote environment management](#open-remote-environment-management).
+1. In **This Computer**, select **Share This Computer**.
+1. Choose a connection method and review the sharing name. Selecting a method alone doesn't start sharing.
+1. Select **Start Sharing** and complete any sign-in or confirmation prompts.
+
+From the client machine, [continue an existing session](#continue-an-existing-remote-session) or [start a remote session](#start-a-remote-session). Sharing enables access to the host's supported sessions, tools, and workspace resources. Review [remote connection security](/docs/agents/run/security.md#remote-connections-and-github-environments) before starting sharing.
+
+To switch methods, select **Change Connection Type** next to **Stop Sharing**, choose another method, then select **Switch Method**. Switching stops sharing with the current method before starting the new one.
+
+To end remote access, select **Stop Sharing**. This doesn't stop running agent tasks. If a sharing change fails, select **Open Sharing Log** in the error message for details.
+
+Turning off ``sessions.chat.remoteEnvironments.sharingSetup`` restores the previous **Off**, **[copilot.github_environments_caps]**, and **Dev Tunnels** controls.

 Right-click a session and select **Delete**. For Copilot harness sessions, this also removes associated worktrees created for the session. A shared worktree remains while another session still uses it and is removed only after the last linked session is deleted or archived.
 
-For Dev Container sessions, deletion removes the container only when no other session or unsent draft uses it, even if automatic idle shutdown is off.
+For sessions in a project's configured Dev Container, deletion removes the container only when no other session or unsent draft uses it, even if automatic idle shutdown is off. Deleting a session in a standalone Dev Container doesn't remove that container or its workspace files. To remove those resources, [delete the container in Remote Environments](/docs/agents/run/agents-window.md#create-a-container-without-a-project-experimental).
 
 To delete an individual chat in the Agents window **Multiple** presentation, select **Delete Chat** in its tab's context menu, or press ``Shortcut: sessions.chatCompositeBar.deleteChat`` while it has focus.
 
 ### Clean up session worktrees
 
-Run **Chat: Open Worktree Cleanup** to reclaim disk space from inactive or leftover session worktrees.
-
-![Screenshot showing the worktree cleanup editor with cleanup settings, an inactivity filter, and selected session worktrees with their storage sizes.](../../images/chat-sessions/worktree-cleanup-editor.png)
-
-1. Choose an inactivity filter and, if needed, a workspace.
-1. Review the selected worktrees, including their branches, paths, and estimated sizes. Clear any selected worktrees you want to keep.
-1. Save open file edits in the selected worktrees, then confirm cleanup.
-
-Normal cleanup marks listed sessions done, commits nonignored uncommitted changes to their branches, and schedules the worktree folders for removal. Branches and session history are retained, so restoring a listed session re-creates its worktree. Ignored files in removed worktrees are deleted. Normal cleanup protects worktrees used by other sessions or chats.
-
-The cleanup editor also includes:
-
-* **Leftover worktrees from done or archived sessions.** Marking a session done does not guarantee that its worktree was removed. Eligible leftovers can be cleaned up even though their storage sizes are not measured.
-* **Managed worktrees without a listed session.** Git-registered session worktrees under the managed `<repository>.worktrees` directory can be cleaned up while retaining their branches, even when storage and last-used time are unknown. Arbitrary external worktrees and unregistered folders are excluded.
-
-Storage sizes are estimates. Unchanged idle estimates are reused across refreshes and restarts; new, changed, or active worktrees are measured on request. An unknown size does not mean zero storage or confirmed removal.
-
-If cleanup fails, review the error and use **Retry Failed Worktrees** for eligible failures. If instructed, restore the session and mark it done again. Failed or unresolved cleanup does not confirm that the worktree was removed.
-
-> [!CAUTION]
-> **Force Remove** permanently discards uncommitted changes and can remove a worktree used by other sessions or chats. Review the separate confirmation and preserve any needed work before proceeding.
-
-Open **Cleanup Settings** to configure automatic completion, deletion retention, or storage suggestions.
-
-**Storage suggestions (Experimental):** Enable ``chat.agentSessions.sessionStorageCleanupSuggestion.enabled`` for a suggestion in the Agents window when inactive worktrees use at least 5 GiB or 20 worktrees qualify. Selecting it opens the cleanup editor. Dismiss it for the current window, or select **Don't Show Again** or run **Chat: Disable Session Storage Cleanup Suggestions** to turn it off.
+Archive or mark a session done to finish its conversation without deleting its history. To reclaim disk space from multiple inactive sessions or leftover worktrees, use **Chat: Open Worktree Cleanup**. Marking a session done doesn't guarantee that its worktree was removed.
+
+For the shared procedure in the regular editor window and Agents window, including remote hosts, preservation rules, and failures, see [Clean up session worktrees](/docs/agents/run/worktrees.md#clean-up-session-worktrees).
 
 ### Automatically clean up merged sessions
 
```

- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/updates/v1_149

### [MODIFICADO] `microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_148.md`
- **URL Canónica**: https://code.visualstudio.com/updates/v1_148
- **Hash Anterior**: `73caa09149dbf845835be68440e0bf6e9bef8f0ef730659036357e07e89611c0`
- **Hash Nuevo**: `6f1c2e024ca3534d67237e0ad573cdb8f26103aae59834d24ee7c60bbf1e8f3a`

```diff
--- microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_148.md (prev: 73caa091)
+++ microsoft_vscode-docs::release-notes/v1_148.md (new: 6f1c2e02)
@@ -1,56 +1,231 @@
-**MetaDescription**: Learn what is new in the latest Visual Studio Code Insiders build, including agent session management and editor tab improvements.
+**MetaDescription**: Learn what's new in Visual Studio Code 1.148
 
 # Visual Studio Code 1.148
 
-Follow us on [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/showcase/vs-code), [X](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=533687), [Bluesky](https://bsky.app/profile/vscode.dev), [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/vscode)
+## Release highlights
+
+Keep agent work moving, even when you step away. This week, chat responses start faster and active agents can keep their hosts awake. Give a task its own Dev Container, and reclaim disk space from old worktrees without switching windows.
+
+* [Keep agents going (Preview)](#prevent-idle-sleep-while-an-agent-works-preview): Prevent idle sleep of the systemon supported hosts while agents work.
+
+* [Worktree cleanup](#worktree-cleanup-on-remote-hosts): Reclaim disk space from inactive agent worktrees on remote hosts.
+
+* [Sandbox profiles (Experimental)](#sandbox-profiles-for-remote-environments-experimental): Tailor sandbox rules to each remote agent host with reusable profiles.
+
+* [Create Dev Containers (Experimental)](#create-dev-containers-for-agent-sessions-experimental): Give an agent an isolated Linux workspace without writing a container configuration.
+
+* [Agent chat performance](#improved-chat-performance): Spend less time waiting for Agent Host chat responses to begin.
+
+## Agents
+
+### Improved chat performance
+
+This week we've made several improvements to the performance of chat in the Agent Host. On average you can expect to see responses begin 30% faster than in our previous Stable version.
+
+We appreciate your feedback as we've transitioned our harness to the [Agent Host](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/concepts/agent-host), and the performance difference compared to the old harness was a recurring topic of discussion. With this release, we see chat start times within ~5% of those of the old harness, and we will continue to find ways to further improve in upcoming releases.
+
+### Prevent idle sleep while an agent works (Preview)
+
+**Setting**: ``sessions.keepAwakeWhileWorking``
+
+Avoid stalled unattended work when the machine running your agent would otherwise idle-sleep. A supported Agent Host can keep its machine awake during active turns, including after you close the window, and release the hold shortly after work ends.
+
+Choose a mode:
+
+* `whenPluggedIn` (default): Prevent idle sleep while the host is plugged in.
+* `always`: Prevent idle sleep during agent work, including on battery.
+* `never`: Allow idle sleep during agent work.
+
+### Inbox navigation and organization (Experimental)
+
+**Setting**: ``sessions.list.inboxAutoAdvance``
+
+[Inbox mode](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/sessions/organize-sessions#_triage-sessions-with-inbox-mode) groups Agents window sessions by what needs your attention. Enable ``sessions.list.inboxEnabled`` to use it.
+
+You can choose where to continue after replying to an Inbox session:
+
+* `auto` (default): Open the next Inbox conversation, or focus the Inbox heading when none remain. With a screen reader attached, stay in the current conversation instead.
+* `on`: Always advance through the Inbox, including with a screen reader.
+* `off`: Stay in the current conversation.
+* `newSession`: Open **New Session** with its input focused, even when other Inbox conversations remain.
+
+## Remote agents
+
+### Redesigned Remote Environments editor
+
+The Remote Environments editor in the Agents window is where you manage the machines that your agent sessions run on. Its redesigned table shows the status, last activity, and operating system of each environment, so you can see at a glance which ones are connected and in use.
+
+**Connect** and **New Session** stay on each row, and other actions, such as configuring the agent run behavior or updating an outdated server, are in **More Actions**. Use **Filter** in the search box to sort the table, or to group environments by type or status.
+
+![Screenshot showing the Remote Environments editor with SSH hosts, a WSL distribution, Dev Containers, a Dev Tunnel, and a GitHub Environment, each with its status, last activity, and operating system.](images/1_148/remote-environments-table.webp)
+
+### Troubleshoot remote connections
+
+When a connection fails, it can be hard to tell whether the cause is your configuration, the network, or the remote machine. Select **Troubleshoot** in the notification of a failed connection, or **More Actions** > **Troubleshoot Connection...** on any environment, to open a page that explains what went wrong and offers actions to fix it, such as retrying, opening your SSH configuration, or restarting a WSL distribution. The page can also collect a diagnostic report for you to review and share.
+
+![Screenshot showing the Troubleshoot connection page for an SSH host where authentication failed, with the connection details, an explanation, a command to test the connection, and actions to retry and to copy a diagnostic report.](images/1_148/remote-environments-troubleshoot.webp)
+
+Learn more about [troubleshooting remote sessions](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/remote-agent-sessions#_troubleshoot-remote-sessions).
+
+### Worktree cleanup on remote hosts
+
+Reclaim disk space on connected remote Agent Hosts with [worktree cleanup](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/sessions/manage-sessions#_clean-up-session-worktrees). The cleanup editor includes worktrees on hosts that support cleanup. Rows and confirmations identify the owning host, so identical paths on different machines remain distinct. Disconnected or unsupported hosts are unavailable for cleanup.
+
+Previously, you could only invoke the worktree cleanup from the Agents window. You can now also access it directly from the Chat view or the Command Palette. Run **Chat: Open Worktree Cleanup** from the Command Palette or select **Open Worktree Cleanup** in the Chat view's menu.
+
+Learn more about [worktree cleanup and its safety constraints](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/worktrees#_clean-up-session-worktrees).
+
+### Create Dev Containers for agent sessions (Experimental)
+
+**Settings**: ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.environments.enabled``, ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.handoff.enabled``
+
+Run agent sessions in an isolated Linux container without writing a Dev Container configuration first. These Dev Containers are Ubuntu containers with an Agent Host inside and keep their files in a Docker volume. You can create them on this computer or on a connected SSH host, WSL distribution, or Dev Tunnel that has Docker.
+
+In Remote Environments, select **Create Dev Container** in **More Actions** of a connected host. To create one on this computer, group the table by type (**Filter** > **Group By** > **Type**) and select **Create Dev Container** on the **Dev Containers** group. Then choose the Dev Container in the Environment picker to start a session in it.
+
+![Screenshot showing the Remote Environments editor grouped by type, with Dev Containers on This Computer and on a WSL distribution, and the Create Dev Container action on the Dev Containers group.](images/1_148/remote-environments-dev-containers.webp)
+
+When you also enable ``chat.agentHost.devContainer.handoff.enabled``, an agent can create a Dev Container from an existing session, for example when you ask it to try something in an isolated environment. It creates the container on the host that the session runs on and continues the task in a new session inside the container, which can report back when it's done.
+
+![Screenshot showing a chat where the agent creates a Dev Container on This Computer and continues in a new session inside it to try the latest Python.](images/1_148/dev-container-handoff.webp)
+
+### Sandbox profiles for remote environments (Experimental)
+
+**Setting**: `setting(chat.agent.sandbox.profiles.enabled:true)`
+
+When you use remote agent hosts, you might want different [sandbox](https://code.visualstudio.com/docs/agents/run/agent-sandboxing) rules for each host. For example, give a build server open network access but keep a shared machine locked down. Sandbox profiles let you save a set of agent sandbox settings under a name and assign one to each remote agent host.
+
+Manage profiles with the **Sandbox: Manage Sandbox Profiles** command, and assign them to remotes in the Remote Environments editor. This assignment is stored on the remote, so every device that connects to it uses that same profile. Organization sandbox policies still apply and the remote needs an up-to-date VS Code Server.
+
+<video src="images/1_148/sandbox-profiles-2026112401.mp4" title="Video showing a GitHub Only sandbox profile being created, assigned to an Ubuntu remote in Remote Environments, and a new session on that remote running curl against github.com with the profile's sandbox policy." autoplay loop controls muted></video>
+
+## MCP
+
+### Support for fullscreen MCP Apps
+
+MCP Apps now offer a "fullscreen" button, which moves the app into the editor area in the Editor window or the Secondary Side Bar in the Agents window.
+
+## Integrated browser
+
+### Set a default user agent
+
+**Setting**: ``workbench.browser.userAgent``
+
+You can now set the user agent the integrated browser uses. For example, set it in your workspace settings to test how your site responds to a particular user agent without changing it for your other projects.
+
```

Upgrade Python before upgrading these packages.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Decisions API`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`langchain_core.decisions`}),` introduces `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`BaseDecisionModel`}),` for typed, probabilistic answers about text, messages, and structured state. Questions support binary judgments (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Noul`}),`), categorical choices (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Choice`}),`), ordinal scores (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Score`}),`), and bounding boxes (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Box`}),`). (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain/pull/41765`,children:`langchain#41765`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Decision-driven middleware (beta)`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ModelRouterMiddleware`}),` selects a model for each run from your configured choices and instructions. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AutoModeMiddleware`}),` classifies configured tool calls and blocks those it judges risky. Versions of these middleware were previously released in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`langchain-typesafe`}),`. They now require an explicit decision model through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`classifier=`}),`. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain/pull/41766`,children:`langchain#41766`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`TypeSafe 0.1`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`TypeSafeClassifier`}),` implements the Decisions API and adds image inputs and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Box`}),` answers. On a default-agent turn, input tokens drop `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`65%`}),` (5,395 → 1,895), validated against our `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://www.langchain.com/blog/how-we-benchmark-deep-agents`,children:`revamped evaluation suite`}),` with no quality regression.`]}),_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`optimizations`,children:`Optimizations`}),_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Lean prompts by default`}),`: The authored base prompt starts empty and tool-usage prose that duplicated tool schemas has been trimmed. Isolated to the default agent’s tool schemas, total description tokens drop `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`43%`}),` (4,005 → 2,302); combined with the empty base prompt and opt-in todos, a default-agent turn’s input tokens drop `,_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`65%`}),` (5,395 → 1,895). Tool behavior is unchanged. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4859`,children:`#4859`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4979`,children:`#4979`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/5009`,children:`#5009`}),`)`]}),`\n`]}),_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`features-3`,children:`Features`}),_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/customization#middleware`,children:`Override a default middleware instance`})}),`: A `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`middleware=`}),` (or subagent `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`middleware`}),`) instance whose `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`.name`}),` matches a built-in now replaces that default in place, rather than erroring on a duplicate. For example, pass your own `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SummarizationMiddleware(...)`}),` to change the token trigger or summary model without disabling the built-in default. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4251`,children:`#4251`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Filesystem tools`}),`: New `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/tools#built-in-harness-tools`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`delete`})}),` tool removes a file or recursively removes a directory (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/3659`,children:`#3659`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/3851`,children:`#3851`}),`); `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`write_file`}),` now overwrites an existing file instead of erroring (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4109`,children:`#4109`}),`); `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`FilesystemMiddleware`}),` accepts a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/overview#virtual-filesystem-access`,children:`tool allowlist`}),` to expose only selected built-in tools (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4325`,children:`#4325`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4698`,children:`#4698`}),`); and reads and searches are tuned for open models — paginated `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`read_file`}),` reports total and remaining lines plus the next `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`offset`}),` (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4540`,children:`#4540`}),`), `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`grep`}),`/`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`glob`}),` return partial results with a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`truncated`}),` flag instead of hanging on large trees (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4063`,children:`#4063`}),`), and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`grep`}),` gains a 1,000-match cap with streamed output and optional context lines (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4570`,children:`#4570`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4706`,children:`#4706`}),`).`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`More prompt-caching support`}),`: Bedrock prompt caching via the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`deepagents[aws]`}),` extra (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/issues/4108`,children:`#4108`}),`), and automatic Fireworks prompt-cache session affinity (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4598`,children:`#4598`}),`).`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`NVIDIA support`}),`: A built-in Nemotron 3 Ultra harness profile plus NIM app-origin attribution. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4192`,children:`#4192`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4455`,children:`#4455`}),`)`]}),`\n`]}),_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`breaking-changes-2`,children:`Breaking changes`}),_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Planning todos are opt-in`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`create_deep_agent`}),` no longer includes `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`TodoListMiddleware`}),` by default, so the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`write_todos`}),` tool, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`todos`}),` state channel, and todo-planning prompt are absent unless restored with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`middleware=[TodoListMiddleware()]`}),`. Removed symbols include `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`BackendFactory`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`BACKEND_TYPES`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`FileFormat`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Unset`}),`. New files store string `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`FileData.content`}),`; older `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`list[str]`}),` content stays readable and converts on next write. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4541`,children:`#4541`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Output format changes`}),`: Empty `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ls`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`glob`}),` output is now `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`No files found`}),` instead of `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`[]`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`read_file`}),` no longer renders a fixed-width `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`cat -n`}),`-style gutter — update any parsers of raw tool output. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/deepagents/pull/4561`,children:`#4561`}),`)`]}),`\n`]}),_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Copy the following prompt into your AI coding assistant to migrate a codebase for these breaking changes:`}),_jsx(Prompt,{description:`Migrate a deepagents codebase from v0.6.x to v0.7.`,icon:`arrow-right`,actions:[`copy`],children:'Migrate this codebase from `deepagents` v0.6.x to v0.7 to account for the following breaking changes:\\n\\n1. `create_deep_agent` no longer includes `TodoListMiddleware` by default. If this codebase relies on the `write_todos` tool, the `todos` state channel, or the todo-planning prompt, restore it by importing `TodoListMiddleware` from `langchain.agents.middleware` (not `deepagents`) and passing it to `create_deep_agent`:\\n\\n   ```python  theme={\"theme\":{\"light\":\"catppuccin-latte\",\"dark\":\"catppuccin-mocha\"}}\\n   from langchain.agents.middleware import TodoListMiddleware\\n   from deepagents import create_deep_agent\\n\\n   agent = create_deep_agent(middleware=[TodoListMiddleware()])\\n   ```\\n\\n2. Backend compatibility shims were removed: `BackendFactory`, `BACKEND_TYPES`, `FileFormat`, and `Unset` no longer exist. Replace any backend factories with concrete `BackendProtocol` instances, and add an explicit `namespace` to every `StoreBackend` configuration:\\n\\n   ```python  theme={\"theme\":{\"light\":\"catppuccin-latte\",\"dark\":\"catppuccin-mocha\"}}\\n   from deepagents import create_deep_agent\\n   from deepagents.backends import StoreBackend\\n\\n   # Before (v0.6.x): factory callable, and StoreBackend with no explicit namespace\\n   agent = create_deep_agent(backend=lambda rt: StoreBackend())  # [!code --]\\n\\n   # After (v0.7): concrete backend instance with an explicit namespace\\n   agent = create_deep_agent(backend=StoreBackend(namespace=lambda rt: (rt.server_info.user.identity,)))  # [!code ++]\\n   ```\\n\\n   Also update calls to use the current `ls`, `glob`, `grep`, and `ReadResult` APIs.\\n\\n3. Tool output formats changed: empty `ls` / `glob` output is now the string `No files found` instead of `[]`, and `read_file` no longer renders a fixed-width `cat -n`-style line-number gutter. Update any code that parses these tool outputs.\\n\\nSearch the codebase for usages of the removed symbols and for parsing logic that depends on the old output formats, apply the necessary changes, and flag anything that needs manual review.\\n'})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(Update,{label:`May 12, 2026`,tags:[`deepagents`],rss:{title:`May 12, 2026 - langchain`},id:`may-12%2C-2026`,children:[_jsxs(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`deepagents-v0-6-0`,children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`deepagents`}),` v0.6.0`]}),_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/interpreters`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CodeInterpreterMiddleware`})})}),`: (experimental) `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`deepagents`}),` now supports code execution and programmatic tool calling through a scoped QuickJS runtime.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`Supports `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`version=\"v3\"`}),` in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`stream_events`}),` / `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`astream_events`}),`. Rather than re-serializing the full accumulated value into every checkpoint, only the incremental delta written at each step is stored — keeping checkpoint sizes small as threads grow long.`]}),`\n`]}),_jsx(Warning,{children:_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Rolling back from v0.6.0 is not supported once threads have persisted.`}),` Deep Agents v0.6.0 changes persisted message history and agent files to `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`DeltaChannel`}),`, which writes checkpoints in a new format that earlier versions cannot read. Downgrading to an earlier Deep Agents version switches these channels back to non-delta channels, leaving existing delta checkpoints unreadable and causing incomplete or incorrect state reconstruction. If you need to roll back, use the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/langgraph/tree/main/examples/delta-channel-dump`,children:`delta-channel-dump recovery script`}),` to migrate affected threads, or discard them, before downgrading. More generally, avoid switching a persisted channel between delta and non-delta representations. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/langgraph/pregel#version-compatibility-and-rollbacks`,children:`Version compatibility and channel changes`}),`.`]})}),_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/profiles`,children:`Harness profiles`})}),`: Register per-provider or per-model configuration bundles (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`HarnessProfile`}),`) that `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`create_deep_agent`}),` applies automatically when a model is selected — system-prompt tweaks, tool overrides, middleware changes, and subagent defaults — without modifying the call site.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/deepagents/backends#contexthubbackend`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ContextHubBackend`})})}),` (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://www.langchain.com/blog/introducing-context-hub`,children:`blog`}),`): A new filesystem backend backed by LangSmith Hub. Most useful for channels that grow large over time, for example a message list in a long-running thread. Use `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`snapshot_frequency=K`}),` to write a full snapshot every K steps and bound read latency.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/langgraph/fault-tolerance#timeouts`,children:`Per-node timeouts`})}),`: Pass `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`timeout=`}),` to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graph/state/StateGraph/add_node`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`add_node`})}),` to cap how long a single attempt may run. Set a hard wall-clock limit (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`run_timeout`}),`), an idle limit that resets on progress (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`idle_timeout`}),`), or both via `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/types/TimeoutPolicy`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`TimeoutPolicy`})}),`. When the limit fires, LangGraph raises `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/errors/NodeTimeoutError`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`NodeTimeoutError`})}),`, clears writes from that attempt, and hands off to the retry policy. Async nodes only.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/oss/python/langgraph/fault-tolerance#error-handling`,children:`Node-level error handlers`})}),`: Pass `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_handler=`}),` to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://reference.langchain.com/python/langgraph/graph/state/StateGraph/add_node`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`add_node`})}),` to run a recovery function after all retries are exhausted. Upgrade Python before upgrading these packages.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Decisions API`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`langchain_core.decisions`}),` introduces `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`BaseDecisionModel`}),` for typed, probabilistic answers about text, messages, and structured state. Questions support binary judgments (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Noul`}),`), categorical choices (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Choice`}),`), ordinal scores (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Score`}),`), and bounding boxes (`,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Box`}),`). (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain/pull/41765`,children:`langchain#41765`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Decision-driven middleware (beta)`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ModelRouterMiddleware`}),` selects a model for each run from your configured choices and instructions. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`AutoModeMiddleware`}),` classifies configured tool calls and blocks those it judges risky. Versions of these middleware were previously released in `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`langchain-typesafe`}),`. They now require an explicit decision model through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`classifier=`}),`. (`,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://github.com/langchain-ai/langchain/pull/41766`,children:`langchain#41766`}),`)`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`TypeSafe 0.1`}),`: `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`TypeSafeClassifier`}),` implements the Decisions API and adds image inputs and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Box`}),` answers.
